金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 11 月 02 日
上週重點訊息
◼ Fed 維持利率不變，兩位委員主張升息：美國聯準會於 10 月 28 日以 10 比 2 的表決結果，
將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持在 3.75%至 4%，其中理事庫克與克里夫蘭聯準銀行總裁哈瑪
克對該決議投下反對票，並主張應升息 1 碼。Fed 在聲明中表示，經濟活動正以強勁步調擴
張，但通膨仍然偏高。在已有兩位委員主張升息的情況下，後續通膨數據將是 Fed 12 月是
否升息的重要影響因素。
◼ Alphabet 自由現金流轉負幅度超出預期：Alphabet 於 10 月 28 日公布第三季財報，自由現

金流為負 90.7 億美元，明顯低於分析師預期的負 27 億美元，同時 Alphabet 維持全年
1,950 億至 2,050 億美元的資本支出指引，顯示 AI 基礎建設投資仍持續消耗公司現金。
Alphabet 該季自由現金流明顯轉負，也引發市場對於科技巨頭資本支出的疑慮，並帶動整體
科技類股出現修正。
◼ 英特爾因 PC 需求疲弱下修第四季財測：英特爾於 10 月 29 日公布第三季財報，營收年增
21%至 165.1 億美元，高於公司原先財測；其中資料中心與 AI 部門營收年增 78%，為 15 年
來最強勁的成長，但以 PC 為主的客戶端運算部門營收則年減 5%。公司指出，由於記憶體短
缺推升 PC 售價，加上企業 IT 預算受限，使 PC 需求轉弱。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線波動較大，中期看好現金流穩健的 AI 受惠企業：本次財報季顯示雲端營收加速成
長與未認列合約持續擴大，AI 需求仍強；但部分企業的資本支出攀升、自由現金流轉弱，加
上 Fed 可能於 12 月升息，短線科技股波動恐將延續。然而中期而言，AI 產業將由全面成長

轉向基本面分化，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健且 AI 投資已能轉化為獲利的企業，風險報酬相
對有利；對高度依賴外部融資、自由現金流持續為負的企業則宜維持審慎。
◼ 5 至 10 年期美債及投資等級公司債兼顧票息收益與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性，加上政府
與企業發債供給維持高檔，可能使市場利率中期仍處於較高水準，但近期公債殖利率回落，
顯示市場避險需求升溫，支撐債券買盤；整體而言，短天期債券面臨未來利率下降後的再投
資風險，長天期債券則較容易受到公債供給增加、期限溢酬及利率波動影響，5-10 年期美國
公債與投資級債券則較能兼顧息收與利率風險。

## Document 093: 2026年7～9月期のGDP（1次速報）結果

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7～9月期のGDP（1次速報）結果.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1 / 2

2026 年 11 月 17 日

調査レポート

2026 年 7～9 月期の GDP（1 次速報）結果

～個人消費が力強さを欠き、4 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長～

調査部 主任研究員 藤田隼平

2026 年 11 月 16 日に内閣府が公表した 7～9 月期の実質 GDP（1 次速報値）は、前期比－0.0%（年率換算   
－0.1%）と減少した。設備投資が前期比＋1.6%と高めの伸びとなった一方、個人消費が同－0.3%と 2 四半期連
続で減少したことなどが下押し要因となり、小幅ながら 4 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった。なお、輸入が鉱物
性燃料を中心に持ち直したことで外需寄与度は－0.6%ポイントと大きめのマイナスとなったが、公的在庫の寄与
度が＋0.5%ポイントとなり、おおむね相殺された。 
過去 3 四半期にわたって潜在成長率を上回る高めの伸びが続いていたことや、設備投資が堅調に増加したこ
とを踏まえると、今回のマイナス成長をもって景気回復の動きが途切れたとは判断できないが、景気が力強さを欠
いていることも事実だろう。特に GDP の過半を占める個人消費に弱さがみられたことは懸念材料である。今後は、
良好な雇用・所得環境に支えられて個人消費は持ち直しへ向かうと期待されるが、消費者物価の伸び拡大や住
宅ローン金利の上昇による家計負担の増加を通じて、消費の減少が続くリスクには警戒が必要である。 
なお、物価上昇分を含んだ名目 GDP は、前期比＋0.4%（年率換算＋1.6%）と、10 四半期連続のプラス成長
となった。物価上昇圧力は依然として強く、コスト増加分の価格転嫁が進む中で、GDP デフレーターは前年比  
＋2.4%（季節調整済前期比では＋0.4%）と、高い伸びが続いた。

（注）内需寄与度、民間在庫、外需寄与度は実質 GDP に対する寄与度（%ポイント） 
（出所）内閣府「四半期別 GDP 速報」

### Page 2

2 / 2

 1 次速報値を踏まえた、2026 年 7～9 月期における需要項目別の動向（実質ベース）は以下の通り。

① 実質個人消費（前期比－0.3%） 
 家計を取り巻く所得環境は改善した（実質雇用者報酬は前期比＋0.3%）が、前期に自動車や
エアコンが一時的な要因によって増加した反動で耐久財が前期比－3.5%と減少した上、夏場
の地震や台風、長雨などの自然災害・天候不順によって消費の機会に恵まれなかったこと、食
料品の値上げや住宅ローン金利の上昇など今後の生活不安につながる話題も多く節約志向が
強まったことから、半耐久財や非耐久財も減少した。ただし、サービスは 9 月のシルバーウイー
クの日並びが良かったこともあり、小幅に増加した。 
② 実質住宅投資（前期比－1.1%） 
 住宅投資に先行する住宅着工戸数が 2 四半期ぶりに減少する中、建築コストの増加も逆風とな
り、住宅投資は 2 四半期連続で減少した。 
③ 実質設備投資（前期比＋1.6%） 
 企業の省力化や生産性向上に向けた投資意欲は強く、特に 7～9 月期はサーバーや半導体製
造装置といった AI 関連投資も好調だったことで、設備投資は 3 四半期ぶりに増加した。 
④ 実質民間在庫（前期比寄与度＋0.0%ポイント） 
 前期に大きめのプラス寄与となったこともあり、民間在庫の寄与は横ばいとなった。 
 なお、公的在庫は国家備蓄原油の減少幅が縮小したことから、前期比寄与度＋0.5%ポイントと
なった。 
⑤ 実質政府最終消費（前期比－0.0%） 
 前期に高めの伸びとなったこともあり、横ばいだった。 
⑥ 実質公共投資（前期比－1.1%） 
 名目では増加したものの、工事コストの増加を受けたデフレーターの上昇に追いついておらず、
実質では 2 四半期連続で減少した。 
⑦ 外需（前期比寄与度－0.6%ポイント、実質輸出：前期比－0.2%、実質輸入：同＋2.5%） 
 輸出は前期比－0.2%と減少した。財は堅調な米国向けや中東向けの持ち直しを受けて増加し
た一方、サービスは前期に研究開発サービスが一時的な要因により上振れた反動で減少した。 
 輸入は前期比＋2.5%と増加した。特に財が鉱物性燃料を中心に持ち直した。ただし、サービス
については減少した。 
 結果、輸出から輸入を差し引いた外需の寄与度は－0.6%ポイントと大きめのマイナスになった。

## Document 097: 2026年・冬のボーナス予測 ～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年・冬のボーナス予測 ～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 11 月 6 日

1/2 
2026 年・冬のボーナス予測 
～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

○ 民間企業の2026 年冬のボーナス一人当たり支給額を前年比＋3.0％（昨冬同＋2.8％）と予想する
（厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」ベース）。冬のボーナスとしては６年連続の増加となるだろう。中
東情勢の悪化に伴う資源価格上昇が企業活動の重荷となるなかでも企業収益は高水準を維持してい
る。人手不足を背景とした処遇改善の動きも根強く、今冬もボーナスの増加が続くと予想する。

○ 11 月６日に厚生労働省から公表された26 年夏のボーナス（賞与支給事業所における労働者一人平
均賞与額）は、前年比＋5.3％と５年連続で増加し、昨夏の同＋2.9％から伸びも拡大した。また、
賞与を支給していない事業所も含む「全事業所における労働者一人平均賞与額」で見ても同＋
5.9％と増加している。賞与を支給する事業所の割合も前年の 73.4％から74.0％へ上昇しており、
企業の従業員への還元姿勢が強いことがうかがえる。

○ ボーナスの交渉では、春闘時にその年の年間賞与を決定する夏冬型が採用されていることが多い。
2026 年春闘では、月例給与だけでなく一時金でも増額回答が目立った。企業収益が高水準にある
ことに加え、物価高への配慮や人材確保の観点から、賞与を含めた処遇改善を進める企業が多かっ
たとみられる。また、月例給与の引き上げが賞与の算定基礎額を押し上げ、支給月数の増加と相ま
ってボーナス増につながる面もある。夏のボーナス増に続き、冬についてもこうした交渉結果が反
映される形で増加が予想される。

○ 毎期型の企業、あるいは労働組合が存在しない中小・零細企業においては、より直近の収益状況・
業況がボーナスに反映されやすい。もっとも、イラン情勢悪化によるコスト上昇が企業収益の下押
し要因となるなかでも、価格転嫁の進展などを背景として企業収益は総じて底堅く推移している。
利益の水準も高く、従業員への還元余力はなお保たれているとみてよいだろう。

○ 人手不足感が強いことも、ボーナス増加を後押しする。企業にとって人材の確保・定着は引き続き
重要な経営課題となっており、月例給与だけでなく賞与を含めた処遇改善の必要性は高い。物価高
によって家計負担が増加するなか、従業員への還元を求める声も強い。こうした点も今冬のボーナ
ス増加を支える要因となる。

○ 労務行政研究所が９月30 日に公表した調査によれば、東証プライム上場企業 175 社の 2026 年年末

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
一時金妥結額は、前年比＋4.2％と５年連続で増加した。製造業が同＋4.3％、非製造業が同＋
3.5％と、いずれもプラスとなっている。大企業中心の調査であり、中小企業やパートタイム労働
者も含む毎月勤労統計とは対象が異なるものの、冬のボーナスも堅調に増加するという見方を裏付
ける結果といえる。

○ 冬のボーナス増加は家計の所得環境にとって好材料であり、個人消費を下支えすることが期待され
る。一方で懸念されるのが物価上昇の影響である。中東情勢悪化に伴うコスト上昇の価格転嫁など
を背景として、今後は物価上昇率が高まる可能性が高く、政府による電気・ガス代支援の効果を織
り込んでも、2027 年初にはＣＰＩコアの前年比上昇率が＋３％台に達することが予想される。物
価上昇による家計の購買力の圧迫が続くなか、ボーナス増加による個人消費の押し上げ効果につい
ては過度な期待は禁物だろう。

## Document 177: 3529力旺 台新

Source PDF: all report pdf/3529力旺 台新.pdf

### Page 1

本刊載之報告為台新投顧於特定日期之分析，已力求陳述內容之可靠性，純屬研究性質，僅作參考，使用者應明瞭內容之時效
性，審慎考量投資風險，並就投資結果自行負責。報告著作權屬台新投顧所有，禁止任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。 
台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

力旺(3529)：2H26 權利金大幅成長、Security 長期機會大 
建議：維持買進

一、2026 年前三季權利金營收
+13.4%YOY，PUF-based 權利
金+927%YOY 
 2026 前三季權利金營收 21.49 億元，+13.4%YOY。 
成熟製程 NeoBit 佔 24.6%，+8.6%YOY。 
先進製程 NeoFuse 佔 70.7%，+12.3%YOY。 
PUF-based 佔 1.5%，+927%YOY。 
MTP 佔 3.2%，+32.5%YOY。 
權利金成長預計將持續加速，主要受惠於先進製程應用的 ASP 提升、客戶新產
品持續放量、PUF 相關部署的營收貢獻增加，以及高權利金費率應用的產品組
合比重提高。

二、2026 年前三季授權金
+38.2% YOY，PUF - based 
+234% YOY 
 2026 前三季授權金營收 12.51 億元，+38.2%YOY。 
NeoBit 佔 11.9%，-39.3%YOY。 
NeoFuse 佔 36.5%，+35.1%YOY。 
PUF-based 佔 31.7%，+234%YOY。 
MTP 佔 19.9%，+22.9%YOY。 
授權業務預期將維持強勁的成長動能，主要受惠於先進與成熟製程節點、硬體安
全、AI 基礎設施，以及次世代快閃記憶體 Flash 應用等領域。

三、維持買進理由  1) 受惠於先進製程應用的 ASP 提升、客戶新產品持續放量、PUF 相關部署的
營收貢獻增加，以及高權利金費率應用的產品組合比重提高，權利金成長預計將
持續加速。 
2) 授權業務預期將維持強勁的成長動能，主要受惠於先進與成熟製程節點、硬
體安全、AI 基礎設施，以及次世代快閃記憶體 Flash 應用等領域。 
3) PUF-based 已貢獻 2026 年前三季營收 12.6%，+252%YOY。應用面包括
CPU、Storage、光通、FPGA、高速傳輸晶片多面向。Arm Neoverse CSS V3
已將力旺的 PUF 列入核心元件。

1 
3597 映興(AVIN)

報告日期：2026/11/17 研 究 員：洪瑞陽 (Ryan Hung) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 資料中心新專案自 3Q26 開始放量，3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ 
+28.8%、YoY +39.6%，稅後 EPS 0.55 元；10 月營收進一步
提高至 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，本研究預估
4Q26 營收 3.97 億元，下半年營運動能明顯高於上半年。 
 2027 年新專案進入完整年度貢獻，本研究預估台達電集團訂單
營收占比由 2025 年的 41.5%提升至 2026 年約 50%、2027 年
約 65%，成為未來兩年主要營收增量來源。 
 受惠新訂單放量，本研究預 2026／2027 年營收分別為 11.8 億
元／17.8 億元，2027 年 YOY+50.8%，新增業務以台達電相關
OEM 線束為主，毛利率將維持一定區間較難提升，預估 2026
／2027 年毛利率 23.9%／23.4%，獲利成長主要來自營收放
量，稅後 EPS 分別為 1.61／3.31 元。

一、2H26 新專案開始放量，營收動能明顯轉強 
3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ +28.8%、YoY +39.6%，毛利率 24.43%，
較 2Q26 維持前期水準；營業利益 2,993 萬元，營業利益率 9.17%，
較 2Q26 的 7.43%提高 1.74 個百分點，稅後淨利 2,156 萬元，EPS 
0.55 元。累計前三季營收 7.78 億元、EPS 0.90 元，營運較 2025
年同期明顯改善。

受惠資料中心新專案持續放量，7／8／9 月營收分別為 9,788 萬元
／1.05 億元／1.24 億元，呈現逐月成長。10 月營收進一步提升至
1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，創單月新高。本研究依
10 月實際營收、既有業務營運水準及新專案出貨進度推估，4Q26
營收約 3.97 億元，QoQ +21.8% ，毛利率 23.65%，營業利益率
11.1%，稅後 EPS 約 0.71 元，下半年營運成長動能持續增強。

二、AI 電力需求成長，新專案放量，2027 年全年貢獻 
AI 算力需求提升帶動資料中心電力密度增加，電源架構朝高功
率、高電流及高可靠度方向發展，HVDC、PDU、UPS／BBU 及
PSU 等電源與備援系統需求同步增加。映興產品已涵蓋 AI 資料中
心機櫃、備援電力模組、基地台機房及電源供應系統等線束模組，
公司產品亦可延伸至大電流與高可靠度電力傳輸應用。

映興在 AI 資料中心的供應模式以電源系統相關線束及連接元件為

主，並非直接供應 CSP，因此營運成長主要隨下游客戶資料中心
電源系統出貨放大。本研究預期 2026 年新專案僅貢獻部分年度，
2027 年除具全年化效果外，出貨仍持續 Ramp，因此預估新專案
相關營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍，帶動台達電集團訂單營收占比由
2025 年的約四成提高至 2026 年約 50%、2027 年約 65%，成為未
來兩年主要營收增量來源。

三、既有應用維持成長，較高毛利產品支撐產品組合 
除 AI 資料中心外，公司既有產品橫跨工業應用、移動載具、車載
電子及儲能等領域。移動載具涵蓋無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩
托車、滑板車及船外機等所需的防水連接器與線束模組；車載電
子則供應車用風扇、OBC 及煞車系統線束；工業應用包括氫燃料
電池、馬達、機械手臂與家電相關線束及代理元件。

公司自有防水連接器累積 93 項國內外專利，具微型大電流、高插
拔次數、IP68 防水及抗火花等特性；OEM 線束則可透過自主運算
工具進行阻抗與結構優化設計，並取得 ISO 9001、ISO 14001、IATF 
16949 及 UL 等認證。

本研究預期車載及移動載具等較高毛利產品持續成長，可部分抵
銷 AI 資料中心新增 OEM 線束占比提高造成的毛利率負向影響。
3Q26 實際毛利率仍達 24.43%，顯示新增訂單放量尚未造成毛利率
明顯下降；惟隨 2027 年 OEM 營收占比進一步提高，產品組合仍
有毛利率些許負向影響。因此映興未來兩年營運型態將呈現「營
收快速成長、毛利率大致維持、費用率下降」，獲利成長主要來
自營收規模放大，而非毛利率擴張。

四、預估 2026／2027 年稅後 EPS 1.61／3.31 元 
2026 年受惠資料中心新專案自 2H26 開始貢獻，本研究預估全年
營收 11.75 億元，YoY +36.5% ；毛利率 23.9%、營業費用約 1.82
億元，費用率由 2025 年的 17.4%降至 15.5%，帶動營業利益約 9,837
萬元、營業利益率提升至 8.37%，EPS 1.61 元。

2027 年新專案進入完整年度貢獻，本研究預估全年營收 17.75 億
元，YoY +51.1%；毛利率 23.4%、營業費用約 2.20 億元，費用率
進一步降至 12.4%，營業利益約 1.95 億元、營業利益率 11.0%。
預估稅後淨利約 1.31 億元，EPS 3.31 元，YoY 約+106%。 
五、投資建議：買進，目標價 59 元 
隨台達電訂單新專案貢獻，映興有新的營運動能，帶動營收與 EPS
正向成長，以 2027 年 EPS3.31 計算，根據歷史區間與同業評價，
給予 18 倍本益比，目標價 59 元

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況 
一、公司簡介 
映興電子成立於 1986 年，主要從事連接線束模組、代理元件及自
有防水連接器，營運據點涵蓋台灣、中國深圳與昆山、泰國及美
國。公司產品可分為三類：第一為 OEM／ODM／OBM 連接線束
模組；第二為代理元件，包括 Sensata 溫度保護元件、日本 Kurabe
特殊電線及 Anderson Power 大電流連接器；第三為自有防水連接
器。公司目前聚焦 AI 伺服器、工業應用、移動載具、車載電子及
綠能儲能五大應用市場。

公司核心能力在於複雜線束加工、防水連接器設計及代理元件整
合。自有防水連接器累積 93 項國內外專利，具備 IP68 防水、抗
火花、高插拔次數及微型大電流等特性；OEM 線束則可透過自主
運算工具進行阻抗與結構優化設計，並取得 ISO 9001、ISO 14001、
IATF 16949 及 UL 等認證。相較一般單純線材加工廠，公司可利
用一線品牌代理權作為客戶導入入口，再延伸至線束加工與自有
連接器，提高單一客戶合作深度。

五大應用中，AI 伺服器主要供應資料中心 HVDC／PDU／CDU 電
源機櫃、UPS／BBU 備援電池櫃、TPS 基地台機房及 PSU 內部線
束；工業應用涵蓋氫燃料電池、馬達、機械手臂及家電所需線束
與代理元件；移動載具包括無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩托車、
滑板車及船外機所需防水連接器與線束；車載電子則供應車用風
扇、OBC 及煞車系統線束；綠能儲能則以太陽能儲能、大電流連
接器及電池櫃線束為主，相關技術亦可延伸至 AI 資料中心 BBU
備援電力應用。

資料來源：公司官網

二、AI 新專案放量，台達電集團訂單營收占比持續提升 
AI 資料中心為映興目前最重要的新增成長來源。隨 AI 算力需求
提升，資料中心電力系統逐步朝高功率、高電流與高可靠度方向
發展，HVDC、PDU、UPS／BBU 及 PSU 等電源與備援系統需求
同步增加。公司目前相關產品已涵蓋 AI 資料中心機櫃、備援電力
模組、基地台機房及電源供應系統等線束模組，因此可隨下游資
料中心電源系統需求擴張而受惠。公司亦已具備 UPS 電池系統、
IT Rack 、PDU 及電池模組串接等產品經驗，可延伸至 AI 資料中
心大電流及高可靠度電力傳輸應用。

公司並非直接供應 CSP，而是透過台達電集團訂單切入終端雲端
服務業者供應鏈。映興供應內容集中於資料中心電源系統內部線
束與連接元件，因此營運節奏與台達電集團資料中心電源業務高
度連動。除了資料中心電源機櫃外，公司在台達電集團既有業務
亦涵蓋 PSU、車載充電系統、基地台機房、儲能櫃及自動化設備
等產品，客戶合作範圍並非僅限於 AI 資料中心單一應用。

本研究估算，2025 年台達電集團訂單營收占比約四成；2026 年上
半年新專案尚未正式進入主要營收貢獻期，因此相關營收仍以
TPS、車用及既有電源產品為主。隨資料中心新專案於 3Q26 開始
放量，7、8、9 月公司合併營收逐月提升，3Q26 營收達 3.26 億元，
QoQ +28.8%。10 月營收進一步增加至 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、
YoY +76.69%，本研究預期台達電集團訂單相關營收成長動能自下
半年起明顯轉強。

本研究對資料中心新專案採 Bottom-up 推估，將台達電集團訂單
營收區分為既有業務與資料中心新增業務，並依已公告營收、既
有業務正常營運水準及後續專案 Ramp 節奏進行推估，而非直接
以客戶占比反推公司營收。由於 2026 年新專案僅貢獻部分年度，

2027 年同時享有全年化與出貨增加兩項效果，本研究預估相關新
專案營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍，帶動台達電集團訂單營收占比由
2026 年約 50%進一步提高至 2027 年約 65%，為未來兩年最主要
營收增量來源。

惟台達電集團訂單相關業務主要仍以 OEM 線束為主，毛利率相對
低於自有防水連接器、車載及部分移動載具產品，因此台達電集
團訂單營收占比快速提升將對產品組合形成一定毛利壓力。不
過，3Q26 毛利率仍達 24.43%，維持前期水準，加上集團既有產能
可因應新增訂單，營收規模放大有助於固定製造成本攤提。本研
究預估 2026／2027 年毛利率約 23.9%／23.4%，營業利益率則由
8.3%提高至 11.0%，顯示本輪成長主要來自 AI 帶動營收放量及費
用率下降，而非毛利率成長。 
三、車載與移動載具高毛利產品及其他業務正向發展 
移動載具產品涵蓋無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩托車、滑板車、
輪椅及船外機所需的防水連接器與線束模組。公司在此領域主要
競爭力來自自有防水連接器，可提供 IP68、防水、抗火花及高插
拔次數設計，適合戶外、震動及高濕度等環境，產品附加價值高
於一般 OEM 線束。公司原有兩輪載具為既有核心市場，近年亦逐
步向無人機與四足機器人等新應用延伸。

考量自行車庫存逐步正常化、無人機非紅供應鏈需求提高，以及
機器狗等新型移動載具應用擴張，本研究預估移動載具中短期營
收可維持中個位數成長，並以約 5～8%作為 2027 年相關業務成長
假設。由於此類產品較多採用公司自有防水連接器及客製化線
束，其毛利結構優於一般 OEM 產品，因此除營收成長外，亦可作
為未來產品組合的重要支撐。

車載電子方面，公司主要供應車用風扇、On-board Charger 及煞車
系統線束，已取得 IATF 16949 車用認證，可切入對品質、可靠度
及認證要求較高的汽車供應鏈。公司現有產品已涵蓋車載充電、
煞車系統及相關電力電子應用，隨全球汽車朝電動化與智慧化發
展，電力電子、充電系統及熱管理需求仍具中長期成長趨勢。

考量車用產品認證週期較長、導入後客戶黏著度相對較高，加上
電動車及高階車用電子滲透率持續提升，本研究預估車載電子中
長期營收成長約 10～15%。部分高階車用產品專案能見度亦延伸
至未來數年，預期車載業務可持續成為公司較穩定、且毛利率優
於一般 OEM 線束的產品線。

工業應用為公司既有營收的重要來源，產品涵蓋氫燃料電池、馬

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保

達、機械手臂、燃油引擎所需線束模組與代理元件，以及空調壓
縮機、吹風機、吸塵器等家電使用的代理元件；客戶應用橫跨燃
料電池、工業自動化與消費型家電。

本研究預期工業應用仍受惠工業自動化、燃料電池及資料中心相
關電力需求，維持穩健成長。不過須注意公司應用別與客戶別分
類並非完全對應，部分 AI 資料中心及基礎建設相關產品亦可能歸
類於工業應用，因此本研究在財測時不直接套用單一工業應用成
長率，以避免與台達電集團訂單 AI 專案重複計算。

綠能儲能方面，公司產品包括太陽能儲能系統、大電流儲能連接
器及電池櫃串接線束，其技術與 AI 資料中心 BBU 在大電流傳輸、
電池模組串接及高可靠度連接等方面具有共通性。隨儲能與資料
中心備援電力需求增加，公司過去累積的電池櫃及大電流連接技
術可同時支援傳統儲能及 AI 資料中心相關應用，提供中長期新增
業務機會。

整體而言，本研究預期其他客戶業務將由車載、移動載具、工業
及儲能等既有應用共同支撐。2026 年在既有應用回升下，預估其
他客戶營收約 5.86 億元，YoY 約+16.5%；考量 2027 年基期提高，
且工業分類中部分 AI 營收已另於台達電集團訂單模型估算，本研
究採較保守成長假設，預估 2027 年其他客戶營收約 6.27 億元，
YoY 約+7%。此部分雖非未來兩年最大營收增量來源，但較高毛
利產品比重有助於抵銷 AI OEM 線束占比上升造成的產品組合壓
力。 
叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估 
一、預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.61 元 
本研究將營收拆分為「台達電集團訂單」與「其他客戶」兩部分。
台達電集團訂單再區分既有業務及資料中心新專案；其他客戶則
依車載、移動載具、工業及儲能等既有應用的實際營運水準與成
長趨勢估算。台達電集團訂單營收占比為兩部分個別預估後除算
得出，而非預先假設占比後回推營收。

3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ +28.8%、YoY +39.6%，較本研究原預
估 3.22 億元高約 1.5%；毛利率 24.43%，優於原預估 23.2%，惟營
業費用 4,976 萬元，亦高於原預估 4,200 萬元，營業利益 2,993 萬
元、營業利益率 9.17%，稅後 EPS 0.55 元。雖然營收與毛利率優
於原預估，但費用同步增加，顯示新增訂單放量後仍需考量營運
規模擴大所帶來的費用成長。

10 月營收 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，亦高於本研究

原估約 1.23 億元。考量資料中心新專案出貨進度優於原先預期，
本研究上修 4Q26 營收至 3.97 億元，QoQ +21.8%。毛利率考量 3Q26
實際維持 24%以上，且產能利用率提高有助於固定製造成本攤
提，預估 4Q26 毛利率 24.0%；營業費用則依 3Q26 實際水準估約
5,000 萬元，營業利益約 4,534 萬元、營業利益率 11.4%，稅 後 EPS
約 0.73 元。下半年營收合計約 7.24 億元，較上半年增加約 60.3%。

本研究預估 2026 全年營收 11.75 億元，YoY +36.5%，其中台達電
集團訂單營收約 5.89 億元，占營收比重約 50%；其他客戶營收約
5.86 億元，YoY 約+16.5%，維持雙位數成長。

雖新增台達電集團訂單以 OEM 線束為主，產品組合對毛利率形成
一定的負向影響，但 3Q26 實際毛利率仍維持 24.43%，反映營收
規模增加可部分抵銷產品組合影響。本研究預估 2026 年毛利率約
23.9%，較 2025 年的 23.6%小幅提升。營業費用預估約 1.82 億元，
YoY 約+21.9%，增幅低於營收，費用率由 2025 年的 17.4%下降至
15.5%，帶動營業利益約 1.00 億元、營業利益率提升至 8.37%。

業外損益預估約-598 萬元，考量前三季實際稅率及 4Q26 有效稅率
35%的假設，預估全年有效稅率約 31.7%，稅後淨利約 6,404 萬元，
EPS 1.63 元。獲利成長主要來自營收放量、固定成本攤提改善及
營業費用率下降，而非單純依賴毛利率提升。

二、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 3.31 元  
2027 年營收成長主要來自資料中心新專案。由於 2026 年新專案僅
自下半年開始貢獻，2027 年除完整貢獻全年外，出貨仍持續 Ramp 
up。本研究考量 3Q26 實際營收與 10 月營收均優，預估新專案相
關營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍；既有台達電集團訂單業務則維持穩
定，預估全年台達電集團訂單營收約 11.48 億元。

其他客戶方面，本研究預估 2026 年營收約 5.86 億元，2027 年考
量車載、移動載具及其他既有業務持續成長，但 2026 已有較高基
期，因此採較正常化成長假設，預估全年營收約 6.27 億元，YoY
約+7%。

兩者加總後，預估 2027 年營收 17.75 億元，YoY +51.1%，台達電
集團訂單營收占比提高至約 65%。相較原預估 16.94 億元，本次主
要反映資料中心新專案出貨進度提前，而非 ASP 上升。

季度營收預估呈逐季上升，1Q27／2Q27／3Q27／4Q27 分別為 4.05
／4.29／4.58／4.83 億元。新專案出貨增加使台達電集團訂單營收
占比逐步提高，但考量 OEM 比重上升，毛利率維持；另一方面，

新增營收有助於產能利用率及固定成本攤提改善，本研究預估四

營業費用方面，本研究依 3Q26 實際費用及 4Q26 預估費用水準進

元，YoY 約+20.8%；惟營收增幅高於費用，預估全年費用率由 2026
年的 15.5%降至 12.4%，帶動營業利益約 1.95 元、營業利益率
11.0%。

業外收入預估約 500 萬元，有效稅率維持 35%，預估 2027 年稅後
淨利約 1.31 億元，EPS 3.33 元，YoY 約+104%。季度 EPS 預估分
別為 0.74／0.80／0.87／0.93 元，營業利益率隨營收放量由 1Q27
的 10.7%逐步提高至 4Q27 的 11.3%，顯示本輪獲利成長仍主要由
營業槓桿帶動。 
三、台達電集團訂單為主要成長，其他業務維持穩健  
台達電集團訂單方面，本研究預估既有業務維持正常營運水準，
資料中心新專案則自 2H26 進入放量階段。考量 3Q26 實際營收及
10 月營收均優，預估 2026／2027 年台達電集團訂單營收分別為
5.89／11.48 億元，YoY 約+65%／+95%，占合併營收比重由 2026
年的約 50%提高至 2027 年約 65%，為未來兩年最主要營收增量來
源。

其他客戶方面，本研究依公司既有應用營運趨勢進行估算，預估
2026 年營收約 5.86 億元，2027 年增加至約 6.27 億元，YoY 約+7%。
2027 年增速較 2026 正常化，主要考量 2026 已有較高基期。

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com

貿聯 3Q26 財 報 優 於 預 期 ， 營 業 利 益 率 創 歷 史 新 高 ， 加 上 
Interplex 併表效益優於原先預估，為後續營運增添新亮點。展
望未來，研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，主要考量：(1) 隨 
AI 機櫃功耗持續提升，電力傳輸規格朝高電流、高壓化發展，帶
動 Power Whip、Busbar 等產品升級，單櫃電源產值可望倍增；
(2) 高速互連朝 1.6T 以上演進，AEC 與高階光纖業務接續放
量，推升資料傳輸產值；(3) Interplex 併購補足精密機構能力，
結合既有電力及高速互連布局，貿聯可望由零組件供應商逐步延
伸至機櫃次系統整合，擴大單一客戶供應範圍及長期成長商機。
研究部將 Interplex 納入預估，上修 2026、2027 年稀釋後 EPS 
至 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）及 145.24 元
（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建
置不確定性升高，可能使新平台導入與機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑
評價表現，研究部將本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍，以 2027 年 
EPS 為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至
「強力買進」。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營業利益率創歷史新高，財報優於預期 
貿聯 3Q26 營收 278.75 億元（+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY），符
合預期。其中 HPC 營收成長強勁（+30% QoQ、+61% YoY），營收
占比由 2Q26 的 42% 提升至 46%。隨 AEC 拉貨恢復正常，上半
年產線未達經濟規模的不利影響逐步消除，帶動整體毛利率回升
至 32.53%（+1.80ppt QoQ、−0.72ppt YoY）；加上營收規模擴
大，營業費用率降至 11.75%（−1.76ppt QoQ、−1.97ppt YoY），
推升營業利益率至 20.78%（+3.56ppt QoQ、+1.24ppt YoY），創
歷史新高。營業利益達 57.92 億元（+44.5% QoQ、+61.0% YoY），
優於原估的 52.50 億元。業外因匯兌損失及利息費用合計損失 
2.30 億元，稅後淨利達 42.90 億元（+43.9% QoQ、+63.2% YoY），
優於原估的 39.78 億元，稀釋後 EPS 為 20.34 元，優於原估
的 19.45 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. Interplex 併表貢獻超預期，上修 4Q26 營收與獲利 
貿聯 10 月營收 118.46 億元（+26.9% MoM、+80.0% YoY），創
歷史新高，主要受惠 Interplex 於 9 月底完成交割，10 月首
度完整併表即貢獻營收 6,700 萬美元，約為併購公告時近 12 個
月平均月營收的一倍。Interplex 為 NVIDIA NVLink Switch 機
殼主要供應商，受惠 AI 機櫃需求強勁，併表貢獻高於原先預估。
考量併購效益優於預期，加上既有業務持續成長，研究部上修 
4Q26 營收至 349.76 億元（+25.5% QoQ、+76.8% YoY，原估 
302.69 億元）。預估毛利率為 32.49%（−0.04ppt QoQ、+0.56ppt 
YoY），大致持平前季；隨營收規模擴大及 Interplex 較低費用
率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.85%，推升營業利益率至 21.64%
（+0.86ppt QoQ、+3.27ppt YoY，原估 20.0%）。預估 4Q26 稅
後淨利達 55.28 億元（+28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY），稀釋後 EPS 
由原估 22.38 元上修至 26.21 元（ +28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY）。

3. AI 機櫃供電架構升級，電源單櫃產值與市占率同步提升 
隨 AI 伺服器運算密度持續提高，機櫃功耗由 GB200 世代約 
120kW，朝 Vera Rubin 世代 200kW 以上發展，電力傳輸規格同
步升級。在既有低壓直流架構下，功耗提升帶動電流需求增加，
Power Whip 與 Busbar 須升級至更高載流規格，推升單櫃電源
產值；隨機櫃功率持續攀升，傳統 54VDC 架構面臨銅材用量、配
電損耗及機櫃空間限制，NVIDIA 已推動 800VDC 高壓直流架構，
透過提高配電電壓、減少電力轉換環節，支援未來 MW 級 AI 機
櫃需求。貿聯除受惠電源產品規格升級，於 Vera Rubin 世代的 
Busbar 市占率亦可望明顯提升，推動單櫃電源產值較 GB 世代
倍增。考量新平台相關出貨預計於 1H27 起逐季放量，研究部預
估 2027 年電源營收成長約 114% YoY；中長期隨 800VDC 架構
逐步導入，既有 Power Whip、Busbar 及高電流連接技術可望延
伸至新一代配電系統，持續擴大供應商機。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. 高速互連升級至 1.6T，AEC 與高階光纖雙軌成長 
隨 AI 資料中心傳輸頻寬由 800G 朝 1.6T 以上升級，高速互連
對訊號完整性、傳輸距離及功耗的要求持續提高。短距離傳輸仍以
銅纜具成本優勢，較長距離則由光纖滿足頻寬與訊號傳輸需求，預
期 AEC 與光互連將持續並存。AEC 方面，1.6T 新規格因材料成本
及組裝難度提高，產品單價與毛利率可望優於前代，預計於 4Q26 
至 1Q27 逐步導入量產，研究部預估 2027 年 AEC 營收成長約 
41% YoY，主要受惠高階產品占比提升。光纖方面，新富生主要供
應 AI 資料中心機櫃間傳輸所需的 MPO、MT 高密度光纖跳線及連
接器組件，受惠高速光纖互連需求成長，月營收較 2026 年初成長
逾一倍，獲利同步改善。中長期來看，貿聯亦積極布局 Optical 
Shuffle、CPO 交換器方案及 Micro-VCSEL 光互連技術，逐步由傳
統線纜組裝延伸至高階光學組件，為後續成長開拓更多商機。

圖一、貿聯-KY 1Q25～4Q27F HPC 營收結構

資料來源：統一投顧預估

5. 半導體設備需求強勁，非 HPC 業務維持穩健 
貿聯 3Q26 半導體設備營收成長 11% QoQ、70% YoY，為非 HPC 業
務主要成長動能。隨先進製程及半導體設備投資持續推進，客戶需
求由傳統線束延伸至設備模組、精密組裝及次系統整合，帶動單機
供應產值提升。貿聯已深入薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備供應鏈，並逐步拓
展至微影及檢測設備，擴大產品應用範圍。其他業務方面，工廠自
動化、醫療、能源及車用等終端需求維持穩健，提供多元營收來源。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

6. 上修 2026～2028 年獲利預估，營運槓桿與併購效益持續擴
大 
研究部將 Interplex 併表貢獻納入預估，上修貿聯 2026 年營收
至 1,069.96 億元（+50.2% YoY，原估 1,021.18 億元），毛利率
估 31.4%，稀釋後 EPS 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）。
展望 2027 年，受惠 AI 電源及高速互連業務持續成長，加上 
Interplex 全年併表，預估營收達 1,710.84 億元（+59.9% YoY，
原估 1,517.46 億元）。隨高毛利 HPC 業務占比提升，毛利率可
望升至 34.3%（原估 34.0%）；加上營收規模擴大及 Interplex 
較低費用率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.2%（原估 13.0%），推升營
業利益率至 24.1%（原估 21.0%），稀釋後 EPS 上修至 145.24 
元（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。展望 2028 年，隨 AI 機
櫃供電架構朝 800VDC 演進、高速互連規格持續升級，加上 
Interplex 受惠機櫃需求成長及精密機構件供應範圍擴大，預期將
共同帶動營運成長。研究部預估營收達 2,136.69 億元（+24.9% 
YoY），稀釋後 EPS 196.20 元（+35.1% YoY，原估 171.03 元）。

圖二、貿聯-KY 2025～2028F 各應用別營收占比

資料來源：公司資料及統一投顧預估

7. 中長期成長趨勢明確，投資評等調升至「強力買進」 
研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，隨 AI 機櫃電力與高速互
連規格持續升級，帶動單櫃供應產值提升，加上 Interplex 補足
精密機構能力，逐步擴大機櫃次系統供應範圍，有利深化客戶合作
關係並提升市占率。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建置不確定性升高，
可能使新平台導入及機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑市場評價，研究部將
本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍。以 2027 年稀釋後 EPS 145.24 
元為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至「強
力買進」。

附錄一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等 定義 
強力買進    預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上 
買進 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10%以上 
中立 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10% 
降低持股 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於(-10%)以下 
未評等 沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

한화에어로스페이스 (012450)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 방위산업 | 2026. 11. 2  
방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감

방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감 
지상방산 매출 1조 2,450억원으로 전년비 41%, 영업이익 217억원으로 전년비 
96.2% 급감하며 영업이익률은 1.7% 기록. 실적 급감에 대해 사측은 올해 주요 사업
의 납품 일정이 4분기에 집중됨에 따라 3분기 인도 물량이 적었던 것과 대전 사업장 
생산 중단에 따른 영향 때문이라고 설명. 단, 4분기에는 당초 계획에 따라 주요 사업의 
납품 물량이 집중되어 있어 실적이 개선될 것이고 대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 내에 정
상 가돌 하는 것이 목표라고 밝힘 
수주잔고 증가세 지속으로 성장 지속 가능할 것 
3분기 말 수주잔고 35조원으로 전년비 12.9% 증가. 이를 통한 지상방산 부문 성장 
지속 가능할 것으로 판단. 참고로 사측은 내년 매출 15% 수준 성장 전망 유지. 하지
만 금리 상승에 따른 글로벌 업종 평균 밸류에이션 하락에 따른 방산 부문 적용 멀티
플 하향 조정 그리고 상장 연결회사(한화오션, 한화시스템, 쎄트렉아이)와 상장 비연결
회사(한국항공우주)의 주가 하락 반영 영향으로 목표주가는 하향 조정 
8월 미 육군 차륜형 자주포 사업에서 시제 사업자로 선정. 향후 K9MH는 미 육군의 
작전 운용 개념에 맞춰 맞춤 조정을 거친 후에 특별한 결함 혹은 결격 사유가 없으면 
최종 계약 체결 예정. 동시에 미 육군의 궤도형 자주포 사업에도 참여할 계획. 뿐만 아
니라 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등으로 신시장 개척 역시 지속되고 있다는 점도 긍
정적 
 Analyst 이재광 
rheejaekwang@lssec.co.kr

## Document 253: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발_5484f7f5

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발_5484f7f5.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적 
2026-10-29

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 640,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 285,500 원 
상승여력: 124.2%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 감소 및 환율 하락으로 시장 예상치 하회 
Key: 4Q26~1Q27 부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 수주 모멘텀 전망 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 엔진&FDC 으로 밸류 매력도 충분. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 시장예상치 하회하는 실적 시현  
3Q26 연결 매출액 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영업이익 8,992억원
(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(9,792억원)를 하회했다. 조업일수 감소
(휴가 10 일+중대재해 4일) 및 환율 하락 영향(450 억원)과 더불어, 임단협 관련 예상 비
용 중 75%를 이번 분기 선반영 했기 때문이다. 상선 수주 빈티지는 중형 선박 기준 ‘23 년 
9.6%, ‘24년 65%, ‘25년 25%, 대형 선박 기준 ‘22년 4%, ‘23년 45%, ‘24년 31%, 
‘25년 20%로, 선가 상승효과 지속되는 중이다. 특히 해양 부문에서 루야 프로젝트의 공정
률 확대 효과 및 C/O로 180억원의 환입 발생으로 전 분기 대비 큰 폭의 이익 성장 시현했
다. 최근 키윗과 미국 내 합작 조선소 설립을 추진하며, 미 군수지원함 초도 2척 수주 및 향
후 물량 건조, 그리고 HII와의 함정 선체블록 하도급 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 64 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 82 만원에서 64 만원
으로 하향한다. 최근 상선 실적 피크아웃 우려에 따른 조선주 주가 급락으로 멀티플 하향 조
정했다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 
FDC 에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지속되고 있는 중이다. 올해 공시 기
준으로 육상발전 엔진 약 1.6GW 수주한 것으로 알려졌으나, 미공시된 약 1.4GW 까지 포
함 시 총 3.0GW 이상을 수주한 상황이다. 캐파 증설 기준으로 ‘28 년 1.5GW, ‘29 년 
3GW 캐파에 해당하는 물량을 이미 대부분 채웠으며, 기존 계획 대비 빠른 준공 혹은 공정
률 확대, 더 나아가 추가적인 캐파 증설까지 고려할 수준으로 파악된다. 빠르면 올해 연말 
혹은 내년 초부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 본격 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 
전망이다. 현 주가 레벨에서 상승여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 3Q 실적 및 임단협 불
확성 해소된 현 시점에서 적극적 매수 의견 및 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)로 제시한다.

## Document 266: AI交易崩跌踩踏！金银铜全线重挫，全周期金跌超9%、纽铜跌近10%，原油三周大跌两成

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI交易崩跌踩踏！金银铜全线重挫，全周期金跌超9%、纽铜跌近10%，原油三周大跌两成.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

美联储加息预期升温， AI 抛售潮引发跨资产去杠杆。贵⾦属遭遇集中抛售，周五纽约期⾦
收跌近 5% 、创⼋个⽉最⼤周跌，期银收跌逾 7% 、全周跌 17% 。 AI 需求预期遭重新定价，
纽铜七连阴、连跌六周，创四年多最⻓周跌。地缘政治⻛险⽀撑美油周五反弹，供应过剩
担忧仍压制全周表现，和布油连跌三周。
摘要：
美联储鹰派预期升温之际， AI 交易退潮引发的跨资产抛售潮正向⼤宗商品市场蔓延，投资者纷纷
削减⻛险敞⼝、平仓拥挤交易，贵⾦属和⼯业⾦属本周全线重挫。美油虽然周五有所反弹，但
供应过剩担忧仍然压制市场，连续第三周下跌的势头不改。
11 ⽉ 20 ⽇周五，⾦银铜继续遭遇猛烈抛售：纽约期⾦和期银分别收跌近 5% 和逾 7% ，伦铜和纽
铜收跌近 3% ，原油则在中东地缘政治⻛险升温、空头回补推动下震荡回升，美油和布油⼩幅收
涨。全周来看，纽铜跌近 10% 、伦铜跌超 9% ；原油连续第三周累跌，以收盘价计，美油和布油
最近三周的累计跌幅⾄少接近 20% 。
这轮商品抛售背后，是⼏股⼒量同时发酵：美联储鹰派信号强化加息预期，美元⾛强、美债收

益率维持⾼位，削弱了⻩⾦等⽆息资产的吸引⼒；与此同时， AI 概念股遭遇抛售，投资者开始削
减拥挤交易、平仓此前押注 AI 基础设施需求增⻓的⾦属多头头⼨，甚⾄被迫出售⻩⾦弥补其他资
产的损失。
铜价的暴跌尤其凸显了 AI 交易退潮的冲击。此前，投资者将数据中⼼建设和 AI 基础设施扩张视为
铜需求增⻓的重要引擎，推动伦铜两个⽉前⼀度逼近 1.5 万美元 / 吨的历史⾼位。如今，随着市场
重新审视 AI 投资热潮的可持续性，这部分乐观预期正在迅速消退。
原油则呈现出不同的市场逻辑：周五地缘政治⻛险重新升温，加上美国感恩节假期前的空头回
补，推动油价从盘中低位反弹，但霍尔⽊兹海峡运输恢复、潜在供应过剩以及全球需求担忧，
仍然压制着全周油价。
美联储加息预期升温， AI 抛售潮引发跨资产去杠杆
本轮贵⾦属下跌，⾸先受到货币政策预期转向鹰派的冲击。
周四公布的美联储 10 ⽉会议纪要及近期联储官员讲话显⽰，通胀⻛险仍然令政策制定者保持警
惕，多位官员不排除进⼀步加息。市场对美联储 12 ⽉加息的押注明显升温。周五盘中，市场定

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

价显⽰，投资者预计美联储 12 ⽉加息 25 个基点的概率升⾄ 96% 。与此同时，美元保持强势，美
国 10 年期国债收益率维持在 5% 以上。
加密货币抵押贷款机构 Ledn 联合创始⼈ Mauricio Di Bartolomeo 分析称：
这反映出⻩⾦⾯临的核⼼困境：当现⾦和美债能够提供较⾼的确定性收益时，⻩⾦这种不产⽣
利息的资产吸引⼒⾃然下降。尤其是此前⻩⾦已经积累了⼤量涨幅，投资者在利率预期转向后
更有动⼒兑现利润。
不过，仅靠利率和美元因素，未必⾜以解释⻩⾦近期如此剧烈的跌势。
德意志银⾏⾦属策略主管 Daniel Ghali 指出，⻩⾦市场出现了⼀个值得注意的现象：虽然价格⼤
跌，却没有观察到与跌幅相匹配的⼤规模资⾦流出。 Ghali 在报告中写道：
换⾔之，问题不⼀定是投资者正在⼤规模抛售⻩⾦，⽽是市场买盘明显退缩，导致有限的卖盘
也⾜以引发价格剧烈波动。
AI 股票抛售潮则进⼀步放⼤了这种压⼒。
High Ridge Futures ⾦属交易主管 David Meger 分析称：

这意味着，虽然⻩⾦传统上被视为避险资产，但在投资组合遭遇整体损失时，也可能成为投资
者最容易变现的资产之⼀。随着科技股抛售加剧，部分投资者不得不出售⻩⾦，进⼀步加⼤了
⾦价压⼒。
期⾦收跌近 5% 、期银暴跌逾 7% ，贵⾦属遭遇集中抛售
贵⾦属成为本周商品市场抛售的重灾区。
⻩⾦周五全天保持跌势，美股午盘时段，纽约⻩⾦期货和现货⻩⾦均刷新 2025 年 8 ⽉以来盘中低
位，期⾦⼀度跌⾄ 3502.9 美元 / 盎司附近，⽇内跌幅接近 5% 。现货⻩⾦周五也⼀度跌破 3503 美
元 / 盎司，⽇内跌幅接近 3.4% 。
“（美联储） 12 ⽉ 9 ⽇加息 25 个基点⼏乎已成定局，现⾦收益率超过 4% ， 10 年期美债收益率超过
5% ，因此，已经积累了⼤量收益的⻩⾦持有者可以锁定收益，等待价格进⼀步下跌后再买回
来。 ”
“ 我们⼏乎没有看到⼤规模⻩⾦资⾦流出的明显证据。相反，⻩⾦价格的剧烈波动源于市场出现
了 ‘ 流动性真空 ’ 。 ”
“ 持续进⾏的 AI 相关股票抛售，正在迫使部分投资者清算⻩⾦头⼨，以弥补其他投资的损失。 ”

### Page 3

截⾄收盘， COMEX 11 ⽉⻩⾦期货下跌 4.61% ，创 3 ⽉ 19 ⽇以来最⼤单⽇跌幅，报 3512.3 美元 / 盎
司，刷新 2025 年 8 ⽉ 29 ⽇以来低位，连跌三⽇，本周累跌 9.47% ，创 3 ⽉ 20 ⽇以来最⼤单周跌
幅。
纽约期银和期⾦同样三连跌，不过跌势更猛。周五 COMEX 11 ⽉⽩银期货收跌 7.41% ，创 5 ⽉ 15
⽇以来最⼤单⽇跌幅，报 41.035 美元 / 盎司，刷新 2025 年 9 ⽉ 9 ⽇以来低位。
全周期银累跌 17.31% ，创 1 ⽉ 30 ⽇以来最⼤单周跌幅，和期⾦连跌两周、最近六周内第五周累
跌。
美国⻩⾦交易商 American Gold Exchange 市场分析师 Jim Wyckoff 预计：
这意味着，尽管短期价格仍⾯临压⼒，但部分市场⼈⼠已经开始关注贵⾦属是否接近阶段性底
部。
值得注意的是，⻩⾦ ETF 持仓⾃夏季以来总体上仍有所增加， COMEX 期货投机者也没有⼤规模
削减此前的多头仓位。这与⾦价剧烈下跌形成反差，进⼀步说明当前市场的流动性收缩和买盘

退缩可能⽐单纯的资⾦⼤规模撤离更加重要。
AI 需求预期遭重新定价，纽铜七连阴、连跌六周
如果说⻩⾦下跌主要受到利率、美元和跨资产流动性冲击，那么铜价的暴跌则更加直接地反映
出市场对 AI 投资前景的重新评估。
周五，伦敦⾦属交易所（ LME ）三个⽉期铜收跌 2.92% ，报 12160 美元 / 吨，创 3 ⽉ 26 ⽇以来新
低，本周累跌 9.25% 。
纽约商品交易所 COMEX 11 ⽉期铜收跌 2.63% ，报 5.477 美元 / 磅，刷新 3 ⽉ 30 ⽇以来低位，本周
累跌 9.91% ，创 8 ⽉ 1 ⽇以来最⼤单周跌幅，并连续第六周累跌，创 2022 年 7 ⽉以来最⻓周跌  。
伦铜周五收创将近⼋个⽉新低。评论认为，本周铜市动荡的核⼼原因，是投资者越来越怀疑 AI ⽀
出增⻓的可持续性，以及铜在 AI 基础设施建设中的需求前景。
“ 未来⼏个交易⽇，价格仍可能进⼀步⼤跌。⽽从时间维度来看，⻩⾦和⽩银市场很可能在不久
之后就会触底。 ”

### Page 4

今年以来，铜被视为 AI 数据中⼼建设的重要受益品种。数据中⼼需要⼤量电缆、变压器、电⼒传
输设备和冷却系统，投资者据此押注 AI 基础设施扩张将持续推⾼铜需求。
这⼀乐观预期曾推动铜价在 9 ⽉⼀度接近 1.5 万美元 / 吨的历史⾼位。
但随着 AI 股票遭遇抛售，投资者开始重新审视数据中⼼投资能否持续，以及 AI 企业未来的资本开
⽀是否能够兑现此前的增⻓预期。此前建⽴在 AI 需求增⻓之上的铜价多头交易因此遭遇集中平
仓。
Benchmark Mineral Intelligence 铜分析师 Albert Mackenzie 称：
这⼀判断揭⽰了当前铜价调整的核⼼： AI 数据中⼼建设确实会增加铜需求，但市场此前可能已经
过度提前计⼊了未来数年的需求增⻓。
Capital.com ⾼级⾦融市场分析师 Kyle Rodda 也指出：
Rodda 还指出，美元⾛强也令铜价承压。
周五其他基本⾦属同样⾛弱。全周，伦铝累计跌超 6% ，伦锌跌近 5.6% ，均连续第三周下跌；伦
镍跌超 3% ，连跌两周；此前⼀周反弹的伦锡和伦铅分别累计跌超 10% 和约 2.5% 。

不过，铜价暴跌并不意味着实物市场已经同步出现需求崩塌。此前中国部分地区的铜现货供应
依然紧张，上海期货交易所可交割库存持续下降，现货升⽔甚⾄创出纪录⾼位。
这也说明，当前铜价的剧烈调整，很⼤程度上来⾃⾦融市场对未来需求预期的重新定价，⽽⾮
现货市场已经全⾯转向宽松。
地缘政治⻛险⽀撑美油反弹，供应过剩担忧仍压制周线表现
与⾦属市场不同，原油周五出现反弹，但全周仍然⼤幅下跌。
周五欧股交易时段，国际原油期货⼀度⾛弱。 WTI 1 ⽉原油期货最低跌⾄ 80.78 美元 / 桶，⽇内跌
幅约 2% ；布伦特 1 ⽉原油期货最低跌⾄ 90.06 美元 / 桶，⽇内跌幅接近 2.1% 。随后油价震荡回
升，美股午盘时段彻底摆脱跌势。
截⾄周五收盘，两连跌的 WTI 12 ⽉原油期货上涨 0.77 美元，涨幅 0.93% ，报 83.98 美元 / 桶，摆脱
周四刷新的 8 ⽉ 26 ⽇以来收盘低位；布伦特 1 ⽉原油期货上涨 0.59 美元，涨幅 0.64% ，报 92.57 美
元 / 桶，连续两⽇收涨，继续脱离周三刷新的⼀周低位。

但周五上⾏并未改变原油全周疲弱⾛势。本周美油累跌 5.17% ，布油累跌 3.41% ，均连跌三周。
最近三周，美油累跌 19.34% ，布油累跌 21.31% ，均创 6 ⽉ 26 ⽇以来最⼤三周跌幅。
“ 只要 AI ⼀直在推动铜价上涨，我们就⼀直在说，这种影响可能有些被过度放⼤了。 ”
“ 越来越多的迹象表明， AI 投资热潮正在减弱，⽽美联储为了控制通胀，将不得不采取更加激进
的政策。 ”

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years.1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.
Exhibit 1: Consumer Spending is Being Supported by Multiple Engines.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026ECONEWS

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by large market swings triggered by
the US midterms results. In the end, energy prices end the week much
lower, bond yields somewhat lower and stock prices down in tech-heavy
markets and slightly up elsewhere, notably in Europe. Incoming data
confirmed a picture of resilient growth overall. The Bank of England

hiked its policy rate by 25 bps and signaled it could do more. Market
pressure on France eased somewhat.
EUROZONE
Energy pushes inflation up, but underlying pressures remain rela -
tively contained. Headline inflation rose to 4.0% y/y in October, its
highest in three years, strengthening the case for another ECB rate
increase in December. Energy remained the main driver, while core in-
flation rose by 2.6%. The ECB wage tracker points to negotiated wage

growth slowing to 2.3% by September 2027. Meanwhile, retail sales fell
0.7% m/m in September and the average interest rate on new business
loans increased 13bp to 3.90%.
To watch next week: September trade balance, Q3 GDP second esti -
mate and employment (Friday).
France
EVs boost activity, market pressure eases somewhat. After already
bolstering household consumption in Q3, new car registrations rose
7% y/y in October (+4% y/y YTD), driven by another surge in electric

car sales (+81% y/y in October; +78% y/y YTD). In parallel, manufac -
turing output rebounded by 0.5% m/m in September, supported by
the automotive sector (+7.5% m/m but -5.5% y/y) and capital goods
(+2.6% m/m and +4.8% y/y). However, industrial production fell by
0.5% m/m (and 1.3% y/y), as a result of a decline in electricity produc -
tion (-4.2% m/m). The composite October PMI was revised down by
1.8 points from the flash estimate to 46.6 (-4.5 m/m), dragged down by

services. This sharp downgrade likely reflects the hit to sentiment from
the October 17 riots; however, precedents suggest activity itself should
not be visibly impacted. Appropriately indifferent to the meandering
journey of the draft budget through Parliament, the OAT-bund spread
narrowed to 155 bps (-10 bps in one week) amid a broad-based decline
in government bond yields across advanced economies; Thursday’s
long-term debt auction, the last of the year, was well-subscribed. Stri-

ving to meet its deficit target of 5.4% of GDP in 2026, the government
announced EUR3.8bn of spending cuts to offset EUR3.7bn in additional
expenditure. The High Council for Public Finance nevertheless sees the
deficit reaching 5.5%. The 2027 budget will start being discussed by
the Senate from next week, in its original version, after the national
Assembly unanimously rejected its amended version that would have
led to a 5.9% deficit.
To watch next week: Q3 unemployment rate and the Banque de France

monthly business survey (Tuesday).
Germany
Industrial activity picks up. New orders rebounded 4.4% m/m in Sep -
tember and industrial production increased 0.7%, with the auto sec -
tor delivering particularly striking gains of 10.5% in orders and 17.1%
in production. Mechanical-engineering orders also recovered (+8.3%),
while pharmaceutical production increased by 8.0%. By contrast, large
orders in aerospace, shipbuilding, rail and defence fell by 10.4%, while

machinery and equipment production declined by 6.5%. October's com-
posite PMI was revised up to 53.4, signaling continued strong expan -
sion.
Italy
Services keep the economy above water as manufacturing slips back.
The composite PMI eased to 50.8 in October, its lowest since May.
Manufacturing returned to contraction at 49.4, its weakest reading
since January, while services strengthened to 52.0 and recorded a fifth
consecutive month of growth. Both sectors reported increasing input-

cost and output-price inflation.
UNITED KINGDOM
The BoE hikes and leaves the door open to more. The Bank of England
raised Bank Rate by 25bp to 4%, its first increase since August 2023, in a
more hawkish than expected 8-1 vote, with Catherine Mann favouring
a 50bp hike. The November Monetary Policy Report sees CPI inflation
peaking around 5% y/y by mid-2027 and upgraded Q4 2026 growth to
2.2%, while cutting the 2027 and 2028 growth projections. The com -

posite PMI meanwhile improved to 52.9, its highest since February,
with new business growing at its strongest pace in nine months and
private-sector employment increasing for the first time in 25 months.
To watch next week: Q3 GDP, business investment, industrial produc -
tion (Thursday).
UNITED STATES
Post election market jitters and a hot jobs report. The Democrats’
unexpectedly sweeping victory in the midterms (the largest wave
since 1974) initially led markets to rally on (i) relief that the President

recognised the results, and (ii) hopes of a swift end to the Iran war
(oil prices and bond yields down, stock prices up). But this was soon
followed by a sharp equity sell-off on 5 November, with the S&P 500
down 4.4% and the Nasdaq down 5.0%, erasing USD2.7 Trillion of mar-
ket capitalization in one day. Why? Because of the realization that vo -
ters strongly supported AI-restrictive measures on the ballot (46 out
of 47) and candidates — for Congress and Governor seats — in favour

of curtailing data centers buildout. By the week-end, however, senti -
ment had turned around again, helped in part by a much stronger than
expected jobs report: payrolls increased by 165,000 in October against
a 70,000 consensus, while the previous two months were revised hi -
gher and unemployment edged down to 4.1%. Average hourly earnings
rose 0.4% m/m, their strongest monthly pace since November 2025.
Manufacturing activity also strengthened, with the ISM rising to 55.5,

and the non-manufacturing index easing to a still buoyant 54.5.
To watch next week: October CPI (Tuesday); October PPI (Friday).
JAPAN
Wage growth accelerates. Scheduled contractual earnings increased
3.7% y/y in September and total wage growth accelerated to 4.6%,
helped by strong bonus payments. Real wage growth remained posi -
tive at 2.2% y/y.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Portfolio outflows. According to IIF data, non-resident portfolio invest-
ment flows recorded USD33.8bn of outflows in October, the largest

monthly outflow since March and up from USD19.3bn in September.
Equities accounted for USD31.7bn, led by USD28.3bn from emerging
Asia, as doubts over AI-investment returns and profit-taking hit North
Asian markets particularly hard. Taiwan and South Korea suffered a
further sharp market correction in the first week of November.

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026

ASIA
PMI: expanding except for the Philippines. The ASEAN PMI rose to 53.1
in October from 52.1, pointing to faster expansion. India remained ro -
bust despite a slight decline (54.7). In Taiwan and South Korea, the
manufacturing PMIs declined but remained expansionary, at 54.9 and
51.3, respectively. Indonesia’s real GDP growth accelerated to 5.5% y/y

in Q3 2026 from 5.3% in Q2 marking its fastest pace since 2013.
China
The PBOC steps in as equity correction deepens. The central bank in -
creased its use of liquidity tools designed to support equity purchases,
including the special relending facility for share buybacks and stake in-
creases and a third round of the Securities, Funds and Insurance Com-
panies Swap Facility, for which 31 institutions applied for more than
RMB100bn. The measures follow heavy stock-market losses between

June and October. The PBOC also raised the reserve-requirement ratio
on foreign-currency deposits to 6% from 4%, effective 15 November, in
an effort to slow the renminbi's appreciation.
Weak domestic inflation pressure: In October, CPI inflation slowed to
0.7% y/y from 0.9% in September. Fuel prices increased by 20.2% y/y,
food prices fell -2.9% and core inflation slowed to 0.8% y/y from 1.0%.
Producer price inflation continued to rise (reaching 5.7% vs. 5.0% in

September), principally driven by higher energy and electronics prices.
To watch next week: External-trade data (Tuesday).
EMEA
Central Europe
PMIs expansionary except in Romania. Poland’s PMI hit the 50-mark
for the first time since April 2025 thanks to export orders. PMI was
also higher in Czech Rep. (55), supported by new orders, output and
employment. Hungary’s PMI fell marginally to 53.1.
Poland and Czech Republic central banks hold and hike respectively,

as expected.
Gulf
PMIs resilient except Qatar (deteriorating further to 46.3).
Saudi Arabia: Massive budget deficit in sight. Saudi Arabia recorded a
budget deficit of USD22.5bn in Q3, narrowing by 5% y/y. But over the
first nine months of 2026, budget deficit reached USD65.1bn, up from
USD48bn in 2025, despite a rather good revenue performance (+2.8%
total; +7.8% for non-oil revenues; -1.3% for oil revenues). The 8% rise in
spending in Q1-Q3 partly due to the war will make it difficult to meet

the 2026 revised target (deficit of 4.9% of GDP).
LATIN AMERICA
PMIs remain weak across the region, with only Colombia in expansion.
Brazil’s central bank pauses rate cuts, whereas Mexico’s central bank
cuts rates whilst signalling an upcoming pause. On 4 November, Bra -
zil’s central bank held its Selic rate at 13.75%, interrupting a series of
five consecutive cuts, amid rising inflation (4.6% y/y in September, up
from 4.2%), a sharp post-election real depreciation on fiscal concerns,

and higher energy prices. The central bank also revised up its 2027
inflation forecast to 4.7% (from 3.9%) and removed forward guidance
hinting at further cuts. Mexico’s central bank unexpectedly cut its po -
licy rate by 25bp to 6.25% on 5 November. This decision came despite
above-target inflation (3.5% against a 3% target), a peso depreciation
of more than 9% against USD since September, and relatively strong Q3
GDP growth (3.2% y/y). The central bank signaled that rates would now

remain on hold for an extended period.
COMMODITIES
Geopolitical relief delivers a dramatic energy-price reversal. Mar -
kets have priced in the reduced risk of short-term conflict escalation
through a sharp decline in prices. Beyond this, however, they lack di -
rection, wavering between positive signals (the resumption of indirect
negotiations between the belligerents) and less favorable ones (in -
creased vessel damage in the strait). Brent dropped 7.6% over the week

to USD96.8/bbl as markets priced a reduced risk of near-term conflict
escalation. European gas prices fell almost in lockstep, declining 7.7%
to EUR87.6/MWh. US commercial crude inventories continued to de -
cline, although more modestly than over the preceding four weeks.
Diesel crack spread’s reaction to US elections has been much more
limited than for crude. EU gas storage ended the refilling season at 75%
of capacity (vs. 80% goal).

## Document 358: LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI  investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

LatAm’s silicon dividend: 
tailwinds from the AI  
investment boom in the U.S.

1 
Authors 
Fancisco Campos 
Chief LatAm Economist

Beatriz Garcia Nunes 
LatAm Economist

October 28, 2026 
Key messages: 
- U.S. investment has broken away from Europe and China since 2023, 
powered by AI-related equipment, software and research spending. 
- The AI CapEx boom demands commodities and merchandise that LatAm 
can supply.  
- Latin America’s export gains are real but concentrated. Copper, computer 
and electronics products provide the clearest links to the U.S. AI buildout. 
- Chile is the region’s most direct copper beneficiary. Mexico captures the 
windfall stemming from increases hardware demand and trade-diversion. In 
these cases, the AI-related tailwinds have undergirded appreciable 
improvements in external accounts. 
- The cases for nickel and zinc illustrate that the dividend is not widespread. 
U.S. imports of the former have mostly returned toward pre-pandemic levels, 
while for the latter bilateral gains partly reflect supplier substitution. 
- While expanding AI use, supply shortages and incentives can extend the 
CapEx boom, its financial viability poses downside risks. 
Another ‘American exceptionalism’ story  
Investment has become one of the sharpest dividing lines among the world’s 
core economic blocs. Since 2024, U.S. gross fixed capital formation has firmed 
up appreciably. Meanwhile, investment in Europe has broadly stalled while in 
China’s it has retreated, the drivers of which are beyond the scope of this report 
(Figure 1).  
The U.S. divergence has largely been an AI story: a burst of spending on 
computing power, digital infrastructure and intellectual property has offset 
weakness in conventional construction and housing. Indeed, total U.S. fixed 
investment is nearly 12% above its 2023 level. Within it, investment in 
information-processing equipment has risen by roughly 57%, software outlays by 
about 35%, and research and development by around 15%. By contrast, non-
residential structures and residential investment sit below their 2023 levels 
(Figure 2).

### Page 2

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Figure 1: The global investment divergence

Figure 2: AI-related categories lead U.S. investment

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Eurostat, National Bureau of Statistics of 
China, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Latin America’s commodity channel 
AI is not merely a semiconductor story. Chips require specialized minerals; data 
centers require electricity and cooling; electricity requires grids, transformers and 
storage. That chain pulls in copper, aluminum, silver, tin, lithium and selected 
semiconductor inputs, along with natural gas and uranium. Latin America has 
meaningful exposure to several of these inputs, but copper has emerged as the 
commodity with the cleanest and most economically significant regional link. 
Copper is used throughout the AI infrastructure stack, including power cables, 
substations, transformers, server boards, heat sinks and cooling systems. Rising 
U.S. copper demand has lifted imports from Chile and Peru, particularly the 
former (Figure 3). The effect matters beyond merchandise export volumes. 
Favorable terms-of-trade have helped Chile generate a current-account surplus 
for the first time in roughly fifteen years (0.2% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-
quarter rolling basis) and have allowed Peru to log the largest surplus in about 
forty-five years (4.5% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis).

28 October 2026

### Page 3

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Figure 3: Copper epitomized the commodity link

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Not every metal is copper 
The broader mining story demands more caution, though. U.S. nickel imports have 
recovered toward pre-Covid levels, but the upswing has not displayed the same 
persistence as copper. Mexico has captured a disproportionate share of the 
bilateral improvement, yet that gain looks more like normalization than a 
sustained AI-driven break (Figure 4). Zinc tells a similar story. U.S. real imports 
stand shy of their pre-pandemic levels, and stronger purchases from Mexico and 
Peru partly reflect a reallocation away from Brazil rather than a decisive increase 
in U.S. demand (Figure 5).

Figure 4: Nickel: recovery, not a boom

Figure 5: Zinc: a substitution story

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

28 October 2026

### Page 4

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

Mexico’s hardware advantage 
The larger non-commodity prize is in computers and electronics. U.S. imports of 
these products have sky-rocketed, while the supplier mix has shifted away from 
China. Mexico and various Asian economies have gained share on the back of this 
double-whammy impulse (Figure 6). 
This helps explain why Mexico’s non-automotive manufacturing exports, notably 
electronics, have outpaced automotive shipments to the U.S. and pressures from 
higher oil prices. The resulting non-oil trade surplus has offset pressure from the 
oil balance and helped Mexico generate a balanced current account (0% of GDP 
as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis). The external boost is meaningful, 
although it should not necessarily be interpreted as bona fide evidence of 
nearshoring, in our view.

Figure 6: U.S. computer & electronics imports going into overdrive

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research.

How much runway remains? 
Quantifying or mapping out a timeframe for the AI investment outlook is beyond 
the scope of this piece or our expertise. Nevertheless, we’d venture to argue that 
the capex cycle appears more likely to mature gradually than to stop abruptly. 
One reason is that the investment case is shifting from frontier-model training 
toward enterprise inference. Capacity that once served development can 
increasingly run commercial workloads for companies. That creates a path to 
revenue even if artificial general intelligence (AGI) crystallizes later than 
expected. It also spreads demand to a much larger corporate customer base.  
A second reason is scarcity. Graphics processing units (GPUs) remain tight, while 
power availability, grid connections, cooling equipment, construction capacity 
and data-center complexity limit how quickly supply can respond. Bottlenecks 
may therefore extend the cycle. The constraint is especially relevant for copper 
28 October 2026

### Page 5

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

and power infrastructure, where projects have long lead times and permitting 
hurdles. 
A third reason is strategic. AI development resembles a prisoner’s dilemma at 
both the corporate and national levels. Each participant may worry about costs, 
safety and control, yet slowing unilaterally risks surrendering technological, 
commercial or national security advantages. The same tension is visible in calls 
for guardrails alongside continued spending. Competition can therefore sustain 
capex even when short-term returns are uncertain. 
We acknowledge the risks, nonetheless. Higher borrowing costs raise the hurdle 
rate for an infrastructure buildout financed partly through debt. More important, 
revenues must eventually justify the installed capital base. The bearish case is not 
that AI lacks value; it is that infrastructure spending may run ahead of 
monetization, compromising some companies’ repayment capacity. Public 
opposition to the development of AI infrastructure and/or adoption of AI 
technologies could also slow down the technology’s roll-out. 
The regional balance  
Summing up, the AI investment boom in the U.S. is delivering a dividend to parts 
of Latin America, not the region as a whole. Chile and Peru benefit through copper 
prices and export volumes. Mexico benefits through electronics manufacturing 
and the replacement of Chinese supply. For Brazil and Colombia, the AI dividend 
is rather muted. Where materialized, furthermore, the dividend supports trade 
and current accounts more clearly rather than improving the domestic 
investment. 
These dynamics should persist while U.S. AI investment remains supply-
constrained and strategically protected. If widespread adoption advances and 
the strategic race intensifies, the impulse can last. If financing costs rise further 
or monetization disappoints, the cycle may narrow. Either way, copper and 
Mexican electronics remain the region’s most visible gauges of the U.S. AI 
investment appetite.

28 October 2026

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
LatAm’s silicon dividend: tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. – October 28, 2026

## Document 405: Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Tracking Europe’s AI transition:  
A new STOXX 600 earnings-call 
framework

1 
Authors 
Michael Kirker 
Economist

November 11, 2026 
How quickly is AI moving from experimentation into everyday business, and 
where are companies reporting tangible gains? The answers matter for 
institutional investors and policymakers assessing productivity, operating 
efficiency and labour demand. Yet comparable evidence across Europe remains 
limited, making it difficult to track the pace and scale of the transition. 1 
We address this gap with a proprietary framework that turns what is said on 
STOXX 600 earnings call into three structured indicators: AI adoption 
intensity, reported productivity benefits, and the impact on workforces. 
Large language models analyse what management teams say about AI’s 
operational role, applying a common scoring framework across companies, 
sectors and countries. Our new approach provides a timely pan-European 
comparable tracker that captures not just whether AI is being mentioned, but its 
real-world impact as the adoption of the new technology unfolds. 
Key Takeaways: 
— Adoption is broadening, with signs of faster progress. AI-use scores rose 
gradually from a low base before strengthening over the past 12 months. The 
increase extends across sectors, although the pace and level of adoption 
remain uneven. 
— Workforce impacts remain limited in corporate disclosures. The 
framework does not show a broad negative workforce signal attributable to 
AI. The aggregate score remains close to the level of no impact on the labour 
force across all of our sample. However, this does not rule out AI displacing 
workers at a future date. 
— Geographic differences persist within the listed-company sample. Since 
2022, German companies in the STOXX 600 have generally recorded above-
average AI-use and reported productivity scores. Dutch and Spanish 
companies in the STOXX 600 have tended to score lower on AI use, although 
Spain has recently gone through a rapid catch up. 
— The technology J-Curve: The findings are consistent with an early stage of 
the AI transition. Companies report increasing adoption of the new 
technology, but transformational productivity gains and extensive workforce 
restructuring remain uncommon.

1 1 Our US team regularly publish on data from the USCB showing the impact of 
AI on businesses, and our German team have recently begun their own 
chartbook. In the UK, the Office for National Statistics publishes on the topic too.

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

Methodology 
Sample period: Our sample begins in January 2022, providing a baseline before 
ChatGPT’s launch on 30 November 2022. AI in this framework covers both 
established and emerging technologies, so the launch of ChatGPT is a reference 
point rather than the start of all corporate AI adoption. 
Timing of observations: STOXX 600 companies report on different financial 
calendars and at quarterly or semi-annual intervals. Their earnings calls also mix 
discussion of completed activity with forward-looking guidance. 
We assign each transcript to the calendar quarter in which the earnings call takes 
place, regardless of the financial reporting period discussed. The resulting 
indicators therefore track when information about AI use and its operational 
impacts becomes public rather than when implementation or the reported effect 
occurred. This provides a common dating convention across the sample while 
retaining differences in reporting frequency and disclosure timing.

Figure 1: Coverage of the STOXX 600 earnings calls

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: Share of the STOXX 600 is based on the current company weight in the index. Data collection for the current quarter (Q4 2026) is incomplete 
as some companies are still to report

Constructing the STOXX 600 AI corpus: We use a specialised AI transcription 
engine to process earnings calls from STOXX 600 companies, restricting the 
analysis to calls for which we obtain complete transcripts of sufficient accuracy. 
In Q3 2026, the latest full quarter, the dataset covered more than 500 companies, 
representing approximately 90% of the index’s current market capitalisation 
weights (Figure 1). In the current quarter (Q4) earnings calls are still taking place. 
Nearly 250 companies with an equivalent weight to around c.60% of the STOXX 
index having already reported. 
Historical coverage typically ranges from 300 to 500 companies and represents 
more than 80% of the index’s current market-capitalisation weights. Coverage 
peaks twice a year because some European companies report semi-annually.  
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 3

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3

### Page 4

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

For examples of the AI’s scoring and justifications, see the tables presented in the 
Annex at the end of this report. 
Summary statistics 
Figure 2 shows the distribution of scores across all earnings-call transcripts in the 
historical sample, without weighting for company size or reporting frequency. 
These are transcript-level distributions rather than shares of companies, and they 
combine early and recent observations. They provide context for the quarterly 
indicators: 
• AI use score: Approximately 60% of earnings call transcripts do not 
discuss AI use. Among the minority that do discuss it, most fall into the 
“Active implementation” range (a score of 4-6 out of 10). 
• AI’s productivity impact: Nearly 70% of earnings calls do not mention a 
tangible AI-related productivity benefit. Among calls that do, scores are 
generally in the medium-impact band, indicating notable operational 
improvements. Transformational gains, scored at 8 or above out of 10, 
are rarely reported. 
• AI’s labour impact: Only around 10% of earnings calls discuss AI-related 
workforce adjustments. Reported changes typically receive scores of 1–
2 in magnitude on the −5 to +5 scale, with a slight tilt towards hiring and 
upskilling rather than redundancies.

Figure 2: Raw distribution of scores across the entire sample

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Note: Results are based on the raw number of transcripts, and are independent of firm size, and biased towards firms reporting more often.

Taken together, the distributions are consistent with an early adoption phase: 
companies discuss AI implementation more often than transformational 
productivity gains or extensive workforce restructuring. A technology J-curve 
offers one interpretation of this pattern, with investment and organisational 
adaptation preceding broader benefits.  
This J-curve pattern is strongly corroborated by external micro-data, such as the 
Banco de España’s business activity survey (EBAE) of Spanish firms, which 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 5

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

similarly indicates that AI has a more immediate impact on upfront investment 
and productivity than on headcount. 
AI use: A broad-based rise, with signs of faster recent adoption 
The new AI-use indicator points to a broadening transition across STOXX 600 
companies, with early signs that adoption has accelerated recently. Scores rose 
only gradually from 2022 and 2024, but the latest observations suggest a 
stronger pace across industries and country groups.  
The rise is broad-based rather than confined to one sector. Technology has led 
much of the sample, while financials show tentative recent outperformance and 
utilities have caught up from a low starting point. Among the large country groups, 
German companies have persistently recorded above-average scores.  
AI use across European industries 
To trace sectoral dispersion, we group companies by STOXX industry 
classification and calculate a weighted average of their scores for each calendar 
quarter, using their latest STOXX 600 index weights. 2  
We focus on four sectors spanning different business models: financials, the 
largest services sector; industrials, the largest physical-assets sector; technology, 
a likely early adopter; and utilities, an infrastructure-heavy sector where adoption 
may proceed more slowly.  
Figure 3 presents the AI use score for these four key industries alongside the 
range for all industry scores (represented by the shaded area). 
• The AI use score is generally low over time but is gradually rising from 
around 1 point in 2022 to around 3 in 2026. 
• AI-use scores are rising across industries, but differences between 
sectors are widening. The common direction therefore points to 
broadening adoption rather than uniform progress: sectors can 
participate in the transition while remaining at different levels of use. 
In terms of the industries of interest: 
• Technology’s early lead has narrowed. The sector’s AI-use score was 
near the top of the industry range through to 2024 but has recently 
plateaued as the broader STOXX 600 average caught up during 2025–
26.3 
• Financials and industrials broadly track the index average. Both sectors 
have remained close to the aggregate AI-use score over history, with 
financials generally a little below average, and industrials a little above. 
But financials show tentative signs of moving above average in recent 
quarters.

Utilities.

• Utilities have caught up from a low base. After recording lower AI-use

As a cross-check, our findings align with the European Commission’s October 
Eurobarometer Flash survey of MSEs and Mid-Caps and the Eurostat annual ICT 
surveys, which show low overall intensity but a similar expansion in service- and 
tech-related sectors.  
AI use across countries 
Companies in the STOXX 600 tend to be large multi-nationals. That makes it 
difficult to pin down where physically the AI transformation is taking place within 
their organisation. Instead, our country comparison is done using two 
classifications of a company’s country: 
• Country of domicile: AI is a transformation technology, and this type of 
change is likely to be driven in organisation from the top down. We 
therefore consider a company’s country as that in which it is domiciled 
(which is likely to be where the headquarters is also). 
• Country of primary listing: While companies can generally list in other 
countries, there tends to be a home bias in their choice of exchange.  
Figure 4 presents the AI use intensity metric by both of these measures of country 
(the company’s domicile and primary exchange listing). Again, the shaded area 
shows the range across all classifications, with the largest countries/exchanges 
singled out. 
German-domiciled companies and the Xetra-listed (Frankfurt stock exchange) 
group record above-average AI-use scores in most quarters since 2022.  
Spain’s AI-use score has risen noticeably over recent quarters and now exceeds 
the aggregate average, reversing its earlier below-average position.

AI labour force impact across European industries 
Despite rising productivity, AI has not triggered the negative employment shock 
feared by the public. Aggregate labour impact scores across nearly all sectors 
hover near zero, with a slightly positive balance over history, and a slightly 
negative balance more recently in 2025-26 (Figure 9). But overall the magnitude 
of AI’s impact on labour remains low. For the STOXX 600 aggregate average, the 
score’s maximum deviation from 0 (no impact) is 0.43 on our 0-5 scale.
